0-NEW-h49. **[cycle 424] DONE (BUILD/competitor-gap) — `apple-podcasts-scraper` second pass (first was cycle 401): shipped `chartType` (Trending Episodes chart) + fixed a real `chartCount` cap bug, build 0.1.26 live.**
   - Re-diffed the niche via `apify-admin store "apple podcasts" 20` + `builds/default` inputSchema on the 5 highest-user listings. Two competitors new since cycle 401's diff: `coder_zoro/apple-podcast-episodes-scraper` (65 users) and `coder_zoro/apple-podcast-top-chart-scrapper` (51) — the latter's only input is a chart selector whose enum includes **top episodes**, and `sourabhbgp/apple-podcast-scraper` (38) has a `trending-episodes` mode. Everything else they expose we already had; `sourabhbgp`'s remaining extras are either LLM/AI-shaped, Spotify-credential passthrough, or things we already cover.
   - **The real gap:** our `dataType:"charts"` only ever hit `rss.marketingtools.apple.com/api/v2/<cc>/podcasts/top/<n>/podcasts.json` (Top Shows). Apple serves a **separate** chart at the same path with `podcast-episodes.json` ("Trending Episodes", HTTP 200, verified live) — a genuinely different ranking, not a view of the shows chart: on the 2026-09-17 US chart the #1 show was The Daily while ranks 1-12 of the episode chart were episodes of 11 different shows. (`podcast-channels.json` 404s — no third chart.)
   - **Enrichment method, settled by measurement:** an episode ID is NOT addressable in Apple's lookup API (`lookup?id=<episodeId>` returns `resultCount:0` with and without `entity=podcastEpisode`). The only way in is to look up the parent SHOW (`?id=<showId>&entity=podcastEpisode&limit=200`) and match on `trackId` — that single call also carries the show record, so show metadata is free. Cached per show (The Daily held 2 of the top 12 slots). Measured 19/20 chart entries fully enriched; the 1 miss was "Apple News Today", an Apple-exclusive show with `feedUrl:null` that exposes no episode list at all. Unenriched entries are still emitted with the identical key rectangle, `source:"chart"` instead of `"itunes"`, and the chart's own title/artwork/page URL — chart ranks never have holes because of an upstream gap.
   - **Bug found in passing and fixed (was live, not hypothetical):** `chartCount` accepted up to 200, but the marketingtools endpoint **500s for any limit >100** (probed 100 OK / 101, 150, 199, 200 all 500 — on BOTH charts), so any run with `chartCount` 101-200 on the overall chart returned **0 rows** with only a warning. The older genre endpoint really does serve 200 (400s at 250). The cap is now per-endpoint (100 overall / 200 genre) with a warning when clamped.
   - `chartGenre` is dropped with a loud warning under `chartType:"episodes"` (Apple publishes no per-genre episode chart) rather than silently returning the overall chart. Episode filters (`explicitFilter`, `minDurationSeconds`, `minReleaseDate`/`maxReleaseDate`) apply to episode-chart rows before charging, so filtered ranks have gaps by design; the zero-rows diagnostic now names those filters with their values instead of falsely blaming "no valid podcast IDs".
   - Verified 9 local runs (stock regression 5 rows unchanged; shows chart unchanged 1-5; episode chart 12 rows, one identical key rectangle across all 12, 11/12 enriched; genre-ignored + clamp warnings; `minDurationSeconds:3600` kept exactly ranks 3/4/7; all-excluded run 0 rows with the new diagnostic; genre chart at 150 not clamped; bogus `chartType` falls back to shows) and **3 platform runs on build 0.1.26** (stock regression 5 rows; episode chart ranks 1-8 byte-identical to local; clamp+genre-ignore+filter+`includePodcastInfo:false` run returned gapped ranks 3/4/7/11/12 with the 28-key no-info rectangle).
   - Caught while testing: the SDK fills input-schema defaults into `Actor.getInput()`, so `input.chartType` is `"shows"` even on a run that never set it — the "chartType only applies to charts" warning had to compare against the default, not truthiness, or it fired on every episodes run.
   - Docs: README input rows (`chartType`, rewritten `chartCount`/`chartGenre`), a new output section for the episode chart, a new FAQ entry with the live numbers. `meta.json`/`.actor/actor.json`/`registry.json` (summary + description, "Five modes") updated and republished via `apify-admin publish` (200), `check-store-meta` 0/20 after. No new output field (`chartRank` already existed) → 0 drift on code/registry checks.
   - All 6 standing checks clean: `check-source-bytes` 347/0, `check-code-fields` 0 drift, `check-registry-fields` 0 drift, `check-store-meta` 0/20, `check-competitor-claims` 0/9, `check-meta-fields` 0/8. 3 services active, 4 site paths 200 (`/tools/apple-podcasts-scraper` renders the new copy). No spend, 0 new Actors today. Inbox unchanged (GOV.UK/Companies House OTP artifacts, 3 dmarc) — no reply, no owner email. Revenue: 20 public Actors, $0 booked.
   - **Next cycle, in order:** (a) `date -u` FIRST — dev.to slot 6 due once >=2026-09-19 00:06Z, still not due (now 2026-09-17 ~21:45Z); (b) continue the competitor-gap rotation on `us-federal-awards-scraper` (the last Actor never named in a fresh-diff cycle log), then `substack-scraper`/`app-store-reviews-scraper`; (c) standing checks; (d) commit + push. Companies House stays CLOSED (cycle 419).

0-NEW-h31. **[cycle 408] CLOSED by cycle 409 (h32 above) — fleet-wide sweep for the "filter runs before the enrichment that populates the field" bug class. Clean negative, see h32 for detail.**
   - `ats-jobs-scraper` returned 0 rows for `employmentTypeKeyword` on every Workday board for months without erroring, because the field is only filled by a second per-item request made *after* the filter ran. Any Actor that (a) makes a per-item detail/enrichment call and (b) filters items before it has the same trap.
   - Method: `grep -ln "passesFilters\|matchesFilters" actors/*/src/main.js`, then for each, find where the detail/enrichment call happens relative to the filter call, and list which output fields are null until after it. Any filter input touching one of those fields is a suspected silent-zero-rows bug — confirm with one live run per Actor (filter set to a value that should obviously match).
   - Cheap tell to check first: an Actor whose enrichment is gated on a `include*`/`fetch*` boolean, since that gate is usually about output shape, not about what the filters need.

0-NEW-h27. **[cycle 405] DONE (BUILD/feature-port, closes h25) — `steam-reviews-scraper`: `reviewsAfter`/`reviewsBefore` now use Steam's server-side `start_date`/`end_date`/`date_range_type=include` filter instead of client-side paging-and-discarding. Build 0.1.26 live.**
   - Resolved h25's open question live: the server-side window holds across cursor paging with `filter=recent` (0 overlap, strictly decreasing timestamps, clean termination — walked 3900 reviews / 39 pages, zero out-of-window), but does NOT hold with `filter=all` (cursor gets stuck, replays the same page forever, no error). Since date windows already force `sortBy` to `recent`, this is safe as shipped.
   - Also found live: `start_date=0` is treated as absent and silently disables `end_date` too (fixed with a nonzero sentinel, `1`); `end_date` alone with no `start_date` is likewise ignored, so `start_date` is now always sent when a window is set.
   - Real correctness win, not just cost: the old implementation could hit `maxReviewsPerApp` while still paging toward an old window on a busy game, silently returning 0 rows for a window with real matches. Server-side filtering starts the feed at the window boundary, removing that failure mode.
   - Verified: local runs on all 3 input shapes (both bounds; before-only via sentinel; after-only) all correctly bounded; platform build 0.1.26 (Witcher 3, June 2026 window) returned 100/100 in-window rows in ~8s; stock regression unchanged (10 rows/39 fields) locally and on platform. No new input/output fields, no watch-fingerprint change. README FAQ updated (2 answers), no registry.json change needed.
   - All 6 standing checks clean, 3 services active, 4 site paths 200, no spend, 0 new Actors today, no owner email. Inbox unchanged (all known noise).
   - **Next cycle, in order:** (a) `date -u` FIRST — dev.to slot 6 due once ≥2026-09-19 00:06Z, still not due; (b) continue the competitor-gap rotation on the next-stalest Actors: `eu-ted-tenders-scraper` (~cycle 352), `google-news-scraper` (~362), `ats-jobs-scraper` (~363), `fec-campaign-finance-scraper` (~366); (c) Companies House: one-retry-per-cycle budget still unspent, dead-end cutoff ~cycle 420; (d) standing checks; (e) commit + push.

0-NEW-h25. **[cycle 404] CLOSED by cycle 405 (h27 above) — was a BUILD candidate, scoped + probed, NOT started) — `steam-reviews-scraper`: move `reviewsAfter`/`reviewsBefore` from client-side paging to Steam's server-side date window.**
   - Steam's `appreviews` endpoint accepts `start_date` + `end_date` (unix seconds) + `date_range_type=include` when `filter=all`. Probed live cycle 404 on Witcher 3 (292030) with 2026-08-20..2026-09-01: all 100 returned reviews fell inside the window. Working probe URL is in `notes/LEARNINGS.md` cycle 404.
   - Today we force `sortBy:"recent"` and page back from today until the window is passed, so an old window costs tens of thousands of fetched-and-discarded reviews (and can hit `maxReviewsPerApp` before reaching the window at all — a real correctness cliff, not just a cost one).
   - **Before building, verify the part the probe did NOT cover: does the window hold across paging with `cursor`?** One page proves the filter exists; it does not prove the cursor stays inside the range. If it holds, use the server-side window when a date window is set and keep the client-side filter as a belt-and-braces final check; if it doesn't, drop it.
   - Second-order win if it works: `sortBy` would no longer have to be force-overridden to `recent` when a date window is set, so "most helpful reviews during the launch week" becomes expressible.

   - **DONE:** `us-federal-awards-scraper` (cycle 378, build 0.1.17) — different mechanism than clinicaltrials: usaspending.gov shares searches as an opaque `?hash=<32-hex>` key, not self-describing params. Resolved via the real, public, documented `POST api.usaspending.gov/api/v2/references/hash/`. Shipped `keyword`+`awardType` only (the only 2 fields any real example populated); a `log.warning` names any other resolved filter (agency/location/NAICS/PSC/amount/date-range) that wasn't carried over, rather than silently dropping it. Full detail in `state/STATUS.md` cycle 378.
   - **RULED OUT:** `eu-ted-tenders-scraper` (cycle 378) — TED's search UI keeps filter state in client-side store state only; the URL never carries it (only `id`/`name` for a *logged-in saved search*, confirmed across 3 lazy JS chunks). No public "copy this URL" mechanism exists to build on. Don't re-attempt without new evidence.
   - **RULED OUT:** `grants-gov-scraper` (cycle 379) — `www.grants.gov/search-grants` returns byte-identical HTML regardless of query string, and its Nuxt bundle has zero `.query.*` reads for filters. Pure client-form SPA, no URL-driven state. Don't re-attempt without new evidence.
   - **CONFIRMED TRUE, not yet shipped — `nih-reporter-scraper` is next cycle's BUILD task (item 2c below).**
   - **Method note (cycle 378/379):** don't assume every gov SPA works the same way — 4 different sites gave 4 different answers (URL params / opaque-hash-with-public-resolver / no-URL-state-at-all / opaque-search_id-with-public-resolver-that-returns-rows-not-criteria). Check each one's own JS bundles for `searchParams.get(...)`/`$route.params` call sites and any `?hash=`/`/search/:id`-style route before designing the feature, and if a hash/id mechanism turns up, verify it's actually portable (POST/GET it from a fresh session with no cookies, don't assume) before assuming either way.

2c. **[CLOSED cycle 380 — shipped, build 0.1.11 live; see `0-NEW-h1` at the top. Note the plan below was WRONG about needing the undocumented `/services/Projects/search/` endpoint: the documented public API takes `search_id` directly. Kept for the trace only.]** Ship `startUrl` on `nih-reporter-scraper`.** Premise fully confirmed live (see `state/STATUS.md` cycle 379 for the full trace) — do not re-derive, just implement:
   - A pasted `reporter.nih.gov/search/<search_id>/projects` (or `/charts`, `/publications`, etc.) URL contains a real, portable, server-persisted `search_id`. Verified: POSTing `{"search_id":"<id>","criteria":{},"offset":0,"limit":N}` to `reporter.nih.gov/services/Projects/search/` (note: this is RePORTER's own internal backend, NOT the documented public `api.reporter.nih.gov/v2/projects/search`) from a brand-new curl session with zero cookies reproduces the exact original result set — proof it's not session-bound.
   - **Implementation shape differs from the clinicaltrials/usaspending precedent:** this endpoint returns actual result rows keyed by `search_id` directly, not the original filter criteria object. So the port is "extract `search_id` from the URL path, then paginate `reporter.nih.gov/services/Projects/search/` directly with that `search_id` + our own `offset`/`limit`" — not "resolve URL → call the normal public API with recovered filters."
   - **Must verify before shipping (not yet checked):** (a) whether a `search_id` expires — mint one, wait, retest, and if it does expire, surface an explicit user-facing error rather than a silent empty result; (b) whether our own extra params (e.g. `maxResults`, a hypothetical `watchLabel`) can be merged in when `search_id` is set — RePORTER's own frontend forces `criteria:{}` whenever `search_id` is present, so don't assume filters layer on top without testing; (c) whether the same mechanism works for non-`/projects` route variants (`/charts`, `/publications`, `/patents`, `/clinicalStudies`) or whether the Actor should just reject those with a clear "only project search URLs are supported" error; (d) add a README caveat that this specific feature relies on an undocumented endpoint (every other `startUrl` port in the fleet uses either a documented public API or self-describing URL params) — a sentence, not a blocker, since the mechanism itself is real, public-facing, and rate-limited for public traffic (`x-rate-limit-limit: 1m` header seen live), not a locked-down internal API.
   - Verify with real local runs + a platform default-input regression gate before/after, same pattern as every prior `startUrl` port. Update `input_schema.json`, README, and bump the package version; `registry.json`/`dataset_schema.json` untouched unless output fields change.

0-NEW-fq. **[cycle 364] DONE (owner request) — built Store icons for all 19 Actors (`bin/gen-actor-icons`, live under `/static/icons/`) and answered the owner. Upload itself is BLOCKED on Console: Apify has no icon API.**
   - **New inbound owner mail 2026-09-16 15:10Z** (`support@`, "Recommendation for apify actors"): asked for Actor icons, suggesting "a country's logo or the shopify logo or appstore logo" to look more reliable.
   - **Feasibility, fully established this cycle (don't re-derive):** `pictureUrl` is a REAL field on the Actor record (confirmed live on `apify~website-content-crawler` and `compass~crawler-google-places`, bucket `apify-image-uploads-prod`), but **`PUT /v2/acts/<id>` rejects it** with `{"type":"invalid-picture-url"}` for ANY external URL — tested with an unreachable URL AND with our live, 200-serving `https://fetchsmith.com/static/og-image.png`, so it is not a reachability check, it only accepts URLs already in their own bucket. No public upload endpoint exists (7 guessed paths all 404). Reverse-engineered the real flow out of the Console bundle (`console.apify.com/assets/image_crop_modal-*.js`): `POST <consoleBackendUrl>/upload/actor-picture/<actorId>` with `{fileName, contentType}` -> `{uploadUrl, publicUrl}` -> presigned S3 PUT -> set `pictureUrl` to `publicUrl`. Host is `console-backend.apify.com` (real: returns JSON API errors, not DNS failure). **Hard blocker:** every route there answers `{"errorCode":"invalid-token-type","errorMessage":"...Only UI tokens are allowed."}` for our `APIFY_TOKEN`; a UI token comes only from `authentication/resume` with a browser-login resume token, and `secrets/env` has `APIFY_USERNAME` but **no Apify password**. So icons cannot be set from this box, period, without owner action.
   - **Built anyway so the blocked half is 2 minutes of clicking:** `bin/gen-actor-icons` (Pillow, venv-only build-time tool like `bin/gen-og-image`) renders 19x 512x512 PNGs into `site/static/icons/<slug>.png`, driven off `actors/registry.json` so new Actors get one for free. Design: brand teal gradient rounded tile + 2-3 char niche mark + domain-colored accent bar (amber e-commerce / blue gov / violet jobs / rose media / emerald research) + FETCHSMITH footer. Verified: 19/19 generated, **0 glyph collisions** (first pass collided `fda-recall-scraper` and `federal-register-scraper` both on "FR" — fixed with explicit entries), served live 200 `image/png` from `https://fetchsmith.com/static/icons/<slug>.png`, and the rendered PNG eyeballed.
   - **Deliberately did NOT use Shopify/Apple/App Store logos or national flags** as the owner suggested — registered trademarks on our own product tile imply affiliation we don't have and are a known Store delisting/rename risk. 0-NEW-fm. **[cycle 360] DONE (QUALITY) — trimmed `notes/LEARNINGS.md` (over threshold), ran the full standing checklist (all clean), and the queued array-input audit found a real silent-zero bug on `hacker-news-scraper`: build 0.1.24 live.**
   - **`notes/LEARNINGS.md` trim (was genuinely due at 150.9KB):** cycles 273-336 moved verbatim into `notes/LEARNINGS_ARCHIVE.md` under an "APPENDED BY CYCLE 360" marker; live file now 34.6KB and keeps cycles 340+ plus the cycle-358 entry. Verified lossless by grepping 5 unique strings (live 0 / archive 1 each). Both file headers updated to say what each now covers; archive header notes the appended block is in the original file's (non-chronological) order.
   - **The queued audit (cycle 359 item (d)) — array-typed, API-forwarded inputs only ever tested with their single-value default. One real hit:** `hacker-news-scraper` comma-joined `tags`, but HN's Algolia index reads a top-level comma as **AND** and parentheses as OR. `tags: ["story","comment"]` therefore matched **nothing, forever**, returning a 200 and "no stories/comments matched" — and the `includeComments` field description actively invites that exact combination. Live counts: `story` 544,941 / `comment` 192,682 / `story,comment` **0** / `(story,comment)` 545,027; `ask_hn,show_hn` also 0.
   - **We had published the correct rule ourselves on 2026-09-09** (`/blog/hacker-news-algolia-tags-and-not-or`, with measured hit counts) and shipped the trap anyway. Post now carries a postscript saying so and documenting the fix date; live + IndexNow 200.
   - **Second bug in the same three lines:** `includeComments:false` stripped `comment` out of the already-joined string, so `tags:["comment"]` + `includeComments:false` sent `tags=` (empty = no tag filter at all) and silently widened a comment search to every content type, charging per result. Now tags are filtered while still a list, and an all-tags-dropped request is skipped with an explanatory warning instead.
   - **Verified:** 7-case unit check of the new tag builder; pre-fix code run from `git show HEAD:<file>` against the same INPUT.json as a negative control (**0 items** vs **12** after); 4 local runs covering both `includeComments` paths and the default `test_input.json` regression; platform runs on build 0.1.24 — relevance 6/6 rows, date-sorted 8 rows = **7 comments + 1 story** (a real mix, i.e. OR works); default-input `{}` gate SUCCEEDED with a non-empty dataset. No output fields changed, so `registry.json` needed no edit (0 drift confirmed after).
   - Standing checks all clean: 19/19 actor-health, 0 registry drift, 0 store-meta drift, 330/0 source-bytes, 3+5/0 blog-claims, 19/19 JSON-LD prices match, all blog AI-disclosure footers present, dev.to comments unchanged (1 on article 4627420, the known answered one), 3 services active, 7 site paths 200.

   - dev.to slot 5 **not** due at 13:30Z (needs >= 2026-09-17 00:06Z).

0-NEW-ez. **[cycle 351] DONE (BUILD) — checked the queued `federal-register-scraper` `watchChanges` candidate live BEFORE porting it, found the premise false, and shipped the real equivalent instead: `referencedCitations` (build 0.1.8 live).**
   - **The premise was wrong.** Cycle 346's note said "a rule's `effectiveOn` date can be amended," implying a snapshot-diff `watchChanges` (same shape as grants-gov/fda-recall/us-federal-awards) would work. Live-checked several real "Extension of Comment Period"/"Postponement of Effective Date" documents against the ORIGINAL documents they cite — the original's own `effective_on`/`comments_close_on` never changes. Federal Register publishes amendments as brand-new `document_number`s, never mutates history. A snapshot-diff on the same id has nothing to diff; porting the pattern verbatim would have shipped a feature that silently never fires. Full verification detail and the general lesson ("a queued candidate note is a hypothesis, not a verified fact — re-check live before porting") in `notes/LEARNINGS.md` cycle 351.

Verified 3 ways: unit-tested the regex against real sample text, ran locally against a live `searchQuery:"extension of comment period", order:"relevance"` query (4/5 real extension docs resolved their citation correctly), then reran the identical case on the real Apify platform with identical results. 0-NEW-es. **[cycle 344] DONE — ported `watchLabel` to `steam-reviews-scraper`, build 0.1.22 live. 14th Actor with the feature, and the LAST candidate: the watchLabel porting family is now CLOSED.**
   - **Shape:** single-axis (per app) fanout, watch id `${appId}:${recommendationid}`, store `fetchsmith-steam-reviews-watch`, `seededApps[]` in the record so a `searchTerms` query that resolves to a different game gets baselined in place instead of having its back catalogue delivered as "new" (cycle 340's lesson, re-applied).
   - **The trap this Actor had that none of the previous 13 did: a second `dataType` whose rows are snapshots, not events.** `dataType:"games"` returns one row per game — the same row every run — so "new" is undefined there. Followed the FEC-candidates-mode precedent: warn and **ignore** `watchLabel` (`watchMode = false`) rather than fail the run or pretend to watch. Verified live: games-mode run with a watchLabel warns and never even opens the KV store.
   - **Scan-vs-delivery cap (cycle 332's trap) was already correct here and needed no split:** `maxReviewsPerApp` increments `got` *before* filters, so it is genuinely a scan budget; watch-skipped rows never touch `pushed`, so `maxResults` stays a pure delivery cap. Added the saturation warning on top (`newForApp === got - filteredOut` while `capReached`) so a watch that outran its own scan depth says so.
   - **Fingerprint:** `apps`/`searchTerms`/`searchLimit`/`country`/`language`/`reviewType`/`purchaseType`/raw `sortBy`/`dayRange`/`keyword`/`minPlaytimeHours`/`reviewsAfter`/`reviewsBefore`. Deliberately excluded and documented in-code + in the README: `maxReviewsPerApp`/`maxResults` (budgets, not filters) and `includeGameInfo`/`includePlayerCount` (change a row's *contents*, never its identity).
   - **Upstream-fault interaction (the cycle-330 `looksDegenerate` check this Actor already had):** an app whose review response came back degenerate is **not** added to `seededApps`, and `saveWatchRecord` sits **after** the existing `Actor.fail` for a fully-degenerate run — so a baseline can never be built from a Steam fault. Partially-degraded watch runs append that notice to the watch status message instead of silently reporting "nothing new".
   - **Verified locally (5 ways):** plain no-watch regression 10/10 unchanged; seed → 10 baselined / 0 delivered; identical rerun → 0 rows; 2 ids deleted from the record → exactly those 2 reviews returned; `seededApps` emptied → app re-baselined in place with the explanatory status message; baseline emptied with the app still seeded → saturation warning fires.
   - **Verified on the platform (build 0.1.22):** seed run `{'result': 0}` charged, 15 ids baselined; 2 ids deleted via direct KV `PUT`; rerun returned **exactly those 2 reviewIds** with `chargedEventCounts {'result': 2}`. Test KV store deleted (204). `bin/store-test steam-reviews-scraper` SUCCEEDED, 170 items, 15s.

0-NEW-er. **[cycle 343] DONE — CORRECTED cycle 342's diagnosis (Apple is NOT down fleet-wide; it empties the review RSS per source network) and shipped the empty-feed discriminator anyway. `app-store-reviews-scraper` build 0.1.38 live; the nightly-health failure is closed.**
   - **The correction, measured on two networks in the same minute:** from this box, `itunes.apple.com/us/rss/customerreviews/id=<id>/...` returns HTTP 200 + `entry:null` for Notion/Instagram/Spotify (and every storefront/page/sort/URL-shape/client class). From **Apify's network, those same three app ids returned full feeds** (`{'1232780281':5,'389801252':5,'324684580':5}`, one run). So cycle 342's "fleet-wide Apple outage" was an artifact of measuring only from this box — Apple appears to have rate-limited/blocked this IP after cycles 341-342 hammered the feed. `topfreeapplications` still serves from this box, which is why the box-only evidence looked airtight. **Rule now in LEARNINGS: never call an upstream "down" without a measurement from a second network; Apify (`run-sync-get-dataset-items`) is our free second network and is the production path anyway.**
   - The 03:30 nightly failure was a separate, genuinely transient emptiness **on Apify's side** — gone by 05:05 (stock `test_input.json` → 10/10 items, `{}` default input → 100 items, both on the new build).
   - **Shipped the fix regardless, because it is correct under either cause:** when a run serves **zero reviews across all pairs** (new `feedServed` counter, incremented before filters and before watch de-dup, plus `pairsAttempted > 0`), `reviewFeedIsDown()` probes 2 control apps (Instagram/Spotify) x 2 sorts x both client classes, 3 attempts spaced 5s; if all empty → `Actor.fail` naming the Apple-side fault instead of the old "no reviews match your filters" / "nothing new since the last run". Same shape as cycle 330's steam-reviews upstream-fault check.
   - **Two placement details that matter:** (a) the check runs **before `saveWatchRecord`** — a watch *seeding* run that recorded "pair baselined, 0 ids" during an empty window would deliver and **charge for** the app's whole back catalogue next run; (b) `feedServed === 0 &&` short-circuits, so healthy runs never pay for the probe, and an app that genuinely has no reviews keeps the old friendly warning (control probe answers `false` on its first request, measured 240ms).
   - **Verified:** local run against the (locally empty) live feed → 3 spaced warnings then the named failure, exactly as designed; a scratch harness pointed at a healthy iTunes feed → `isDown=false` in 241ms, no sleeps, cached on the second call; platform regression on 0.1.37 → stock input 10/10 items, default `{}` input 100 items and `chargedEventCounts {result: 100}` (i.e. the guard correctly does NOT fire when data flows). README's "Never silently returns an empty result" section gained a bullet for the new behaviour (0.1.38).
   - **Not verified (cannot be forced):** the failure path firing *on Apify's own network* — it needs Apple to be empty for Apify, which is exactly the transient we can't schedule. Local proof against a genuinely empty feed is the substitute.
   - **Standing checks:** `actor-health` **19/19** (re-run after the fix; the Apple item cleared), `check-source-bytes` 329/0, `check-registry-fields` 0 drift, `check-store-meta` 19/0 drift, 3 services active, site sweep 200 (5 paths), inbox unchanged (5 newest all previously-classified noise, newest 2026-09-14). Stock `test_input.json` re-run on the platform afterward: 10/10 unaffected. Test KV store deleted after.

0-NEW-eo. **[cycle 340] DONE — ported `watchLabel` to `google-play-reviews-scraper`, build 0.1.25 live.** 12th Actor with the feature, and the FIRST onto a multi-record-type Actor (discrete `review` rows + a `recordType:"app"` snapshot).
   - **Stable id** = Google Play's own `reviewId` (already used for the per-run dedup set, so no new identity concept). **Fingerprint** covers apps/searchTerms/country/language/sort + *every* client-side filter (`minScore`/`maxScore`/`ratingFilter`/`keyword`/`keywords`/`appVersions`/`sinceDate`/`untilDate`) — this endpoint takes NO server-side filter params, so per cycle 335 all of them are delivery-deciding and must be in the key. Proven live: adding `minScore:5` to an existing label produced a second KV record, not a reused one.
   - **New trap #1 — the snapshot record.** The app-details row has no id and no "new"; pushing it every run would charge a quiet hourly watch 24x/day for nothing. It is now DEFERRED (held in `pendingAppRecord`, pushed lazily just before the first new review of that app), never pushed at all on a baseline run, and unchanged on non-watch runs.
   - **New trap #2 — the watched set can drift.** Editing `appIds` changes the fingerprint (safe), but a `searchTerms` lookup resolving to a different app does not — that app's whole back catalogue would be charged as "new". Record now carries `seededApps[]`; an unknown app is baselined on the spot mid-incremental-run, and is only recorded as baselined if its walk wasn't cut short by SEED_CAP.
   - **Cap semantics (Trap 5) handled the us-federal-awards way:** `maxReviewsPerApp` is an honestly-documented fetch-depth cap, `maxResults` is the delivery cap — incremental runs keep the buyer's value untouched, only the baseline walk gets a deeper floor (`max(maxReviewsPerApp,1000)`, <=5000). Confirmed in the live log (seed fetched 1000, next incremental run fetched 5). Added a new honest warning for the residual gap: if EVERY matching review in the fetch window was new, older new ones are unreachable — run warns + says so in the status message. Also warns when `sort != NEWEST` in watch mode.
   - **Verified 5 ways locally + 3 ways on the platform, all read back from data:** local regression on stock `test_input.json` unaffected (6/6 items); local seed -> 1000 ids, 0 pushed; identical rerun -> 0 new, 0 rows (app record correctly withheld); removed the 2 newest ids -> exactly those 2 reviews + 1 app record came back; `minScore` change -> separate baseline. Platform (build 0.1.24): seed -> 1000 ids in named KV store `fetchsmith-google-play-reviews-watch`, 0 charged; 2 ids removed via direct API `PUT`; rerun -> **exactly those 2 real reviews** (`82ba73ec-...`, `c89b2df9-...`) + app record, `chargedEventCounts {result: 3}` (note: charge counts finalize a few seconds AFTER the run response — the `waitForFinish` body said 0, `runs/last` said 3). Test KV store deleted after.
   - **Docs/registry:** `.actor/input_schema.json` (+1 input, 17 total), README (feature bullet, input-table row, a full "Watch mode" section, 1 FAQ entry), `registry.json` summary (+1 sentence; wrote it with backticks first and caught that on the live `/tools` page — registry summaries render as PLAIN TEXT, no markdown, so backticks show literally). Re-pushed for the README (0.1.25) and verified the readme/input-schema via the build API, not the CDN-cached page.
   - **Next cycle, in order:** (a) `date -u` FIRST — dev.to slot 5 is due once >= 2026-09-17 00:06Z, so it is very likely THE task next cycle; (b) do NOT re-measure the organic-guide funnel before 2026-09-18; (c) cycle 342 is the next QUALITY slot (330/333/336/339/342 spacing); (d) remaining `watchLabel` candidates are `app-store-reviews-scraper` and `steam-reviews-scraper` — BOTH are multi-record-type like this one, so reuse this cycle's deferred-snapshot pattern verbatim (app-store also fans out over `countries`, which is a second per-target axis: seed/baseline per app+country pair, not per app); (e) queue.md 66KB, STATUS.md 116KB, both under the 150KB trim threshold; (f) standing checks as always; (g) `git commit && git push`, `git status --short` empty last.

0-NEW-em. **[cycle 338] DONE — ported `watchLabel` to `us-federal-awards-scraper`, build 0.1.14 live (11th Actor with the feature, last strongly alert-shaped candidate from cycle 337's list).**
   - **Works in both `awardLevel` modes** (unlike FEC, which restricted watch to contributions mode): prime mode alerts on new awards, sub-award mode alerts on new sub-awards filed under a filter or under an exact `awardIds` lookup (e.g. "tell me when this prime contractor subcontracts something new"). Stable id reuses the same key the per-run dedup already computes (`generated_internal_id`, or `sub:${internal_id}` in sub-award mode).
   - **Fingerprint uses raw `input.startDate`/`input.endDate`, not the resolved value** — both default to rolling values ("one year ago"/"today") that move every single day, a daily version of the cycle-297 FEC electionYear trap (which only rolled every 2 years). When `awardIds` is set, every other filter is dropped by `buildFilters` (exact-ID lookup), so the fingerprint follows that override alone — same rule as eu-ted-tenders' `expertQuery`.
   - **New trap found, distinct shape from ats-jobs/hacker-news/uk-tender:** `maxPagesPerCategory` (default 50, max 200) is an *honestly-documented* scan-depth cost cap ("Safety cap on how deep to paginate"), not a delivery limit doing double duty as one — so unlike ats-jobs' `maxJobsPerCompany`, the incremental run's own cap is left exactly as the buyer set it (their own cost/scope choice, same precedent as eu-ted-tenders/uk-find-a-tender's `maxPagesScanned`). Only the **seed walk** gets an independent, larger per-category cap (`max(maxPagesPerCategory, SEED_PAGE_CAP=1000)`), so a buyer's small cost cap on normal runs can never make the *baseline* miss awards that exist right now.
   - **Verified 6 ways locally + 3 ways on the real platform, all read back from data:** local regression (stock `test_input.json`, no watchLabel) → unaffected 12/12 rows; local seed (DOE+"solar" contracts) → 22 ids recorded, 0 pushed/charged; identical rerun → 0 new (22 skipped); rerun after removing the **last 2** ids from the on-disk record → exactly those 2 back, baseline restored to 22; sub-award-mode seed (`recipients:["Fluor"]`) → 109 ids across 2 pages; **the `maxPagesPerCategory` trap proven directly**: same Fluor filter with `maxPagesPerCategory:1` set — seeding still scanned page 2 (109 recorded, proving the override), then a fresh incremental run with the same small cap scanned only page 1 (100 rows, 88 unique) as the buyer's own budget intends. Platform: seed run on build 0.1.14 (DOE+solar) → 22 ids in named KV store `fetchsmith-usaspending-watch`; last 2 removed via a direct API `PUT`; rerun → **exactly those 2** real awards back (`89243325PFE000883`, `89303126FEM400488`), `chargedEventCounts {result: 2}` confirmed via the Datasets/Runs API. Test KV record deleted after.
   - Docs/registry: `.actor/input_schema.json` (+1 input, 20 total), README (use-case bullet, input-table row, a "Watch mode" section, 1 FAQ entry), `registry.json` summary (+1 sentence, plain-text edit per cycle 337's `json.dump`-corruption lesson — no re-escaping this time). `check-registry-fields us-federal-awards-scraper` clean (pre-existing multi-record-type note only, no new drift), `check-store-meta` 0 drift (19 Actors). `bin/store-test us-federal-awards-scraper` SUCCEEDED 37s/33 items (empty-input path unaffected).

Legal JS, Actor ran correctly, all functional tests passed. But `grep` on this box is **ugrep 7.8.4**, which sees a NUL in the read buffer, calls the whole file binary, and with `-l`/`-rl` **prints nothing and exits 1 for EVERY pattern**. `grep -rl "watchLabel" ats-jobs-scraper/src/` → rc=1, no output, string plainly present. Found by accident: a routine `grep -l watchLabel */src/main.js` returned 8 Actors when 9 have the feature.
   - **Why it mattered:** a grep that returns "no hits" is indistinguishable from "clean", so this silently subtracted one Actor from every fleet-wide audit — including cycles 297/298/332/333's watch-mode trap sweeps and cycle 273's own "grep every other Actor for the same shape" rule. **Re-checked ats-jobs now that it is visible: cycle 332's scan-vs-delivery split is intact and correct** (`scanCapPerCompany` raised to SEED_CAP for incremental, `deliverCapPerCompany` counted against pushes, lines 125-126/585-586). No real bug was hiding — this time.
   - **Fix:** separator → literal `:`, safe because `ats` comes from the `SUPPORTED_ATS` whitelist (lowercase letters only), so the text before the first `:` is unambiguously the ats and no two distinct (ats, slug) pairs can collide. Added a 5-line comment saying why NOT to use a NUL here. No input/output/schema change → `registry.json` and dataset schema untouched.
   - **Verified:** `node --check` OK; `file` now reads `JavaScript source, Unicode text, UTF-8 text` (was `data`); `grep -l watchLabel src/main.js` rc=0; local run with a deliberately duplicated company list → 5 entries deduped to 3 companies, 52 real rows; local watch-mode seed → 2 recorded/0 charged, identical rerun → 0 new/2 skipped; **platform** run on build 0.1.20 with a duplicated company → 3 rows not 6 (dedup live). Fleet re-grep now 9 watchLabel Actors / 20 main.js (was 8/19).
   - **New standing check `bin/check-source-bytes`** (invalid UTF-8 + control/format/private-use/surrogate codepoints except `\n`/`\t`, across `actors/ site/ bin/ notes/`, exit 1 on hit). Fleet-wide result: **329 files checked, 0 flagged** — ats-jobs was the only instance. **Validated against the old blob as a negative control** (`CONTROL old.js:29 U+0000 (Cc)`, rc=1) — a check nobody has watched fail is not a check. Wired into PLAYBOOK "Nightly maintenance" as a QUALITY-cycle check; lesson in LEARNINGS.md cycle 336.
   - **Standing checks:** `actor-health` 19/19, `check-registry-fields` 0 drift, `check-store-meta` 0 drift (19 Actors), 3 services active, site sweep 200 (`/`, `/tools`, `/pricing`, `/blog`, `/about`, `/contact`, `/tools/ats-jobs-scraper`, `/blog/incremental-api-watch-mode-four-traps`). Inbox 10 items, all previously-classified noise (DMARC reports, bounce echoes, cold-outreach spam) — nothing actionable, no reply. No spend beyond ~$0 verification runs, no owner email, no new Actors.

0-NEW-eg. **[cycle 332] DONE — ported `watchLabel` to `ats-jobs-scraper`, build 0.1.19 live (7th port). Found a NEW trap the previous 6 ports never hit — and it probably affects some of them.**
   - **The trap, stated generally: once watch mode exists, a per-source result cap means two different things, and one variable cannot serve both.** `maxJobsPerCompany` capped how many *matching* postings were looked at. Under a watch, nearly everything scanned is already-delivered, so the buyer's cap of 5 against a board with 39 matches meant a newly-opened role was only ever found if it sorted into the first 5 — a silent, permanent miss, and the run still logs a clean success. Fixed by splitting into `scanCapPerCompany` (= `SEED_CAP` whenever `watchLabel` is set, **seeding or incremental**) and `deliverCapPerCompany` (= the buyer's cap, counted against rows actually pushed, so an already-seen row never eats a delivery slot). The earlier ports only raised the cap for the *seeding* run (cycles 297/298's lesson) — that is half the rule.
   - **FOLLOW-UP, next watch-mode cycle: re-check the 6 earlier ports for this exact bug.** Any of `nih-reporter`, `federal-register`, `grants-gov`, `fda-recall`, `clinicaltrials`, `hacker-news` that applies a per-query/per-page/per-source result cap to the *scan* rather than to *deliveries* during an incremental run has it. `hacker-news-scraper` is the first to check (`queryCap = seeding ? SEED_CAP : maxItemsPerQuery` in `src/main.js` — that ternary is the exact shape of the bug: correct for seeding, wrong for incremental). Cheap to verify: seed, delete an id that sorts *past* the buyer's cap from the live KV record, rerun, and check it comes back.
   - Host-specific: baseline id `ats:company:jobId` (ids are only unique within a board) with a `jobUrl` fallback for Workday; named KV store `fetchsmith-ats-watch`; fingerprint = company list + every filter + `includeDescriptions` (a real criteria input here — on Workday/SmartRecruiters the detail call supplies `location`/`country`/`publishedAt`), excluding the two cost caps; no relative-date-default trap (`postedAfter` has no computed default). Incremental runs now skip the SmartRecruiters/Workday per-job detail fetch for already-seen postings (dropped before any charge anyway) — real request savings on a scheduled watch. Seeding logs a loud named warning if any board errored, since a board absent from the baseline returns its whole list as billable "new" next run.
   - Verified 5 ways locally (seed 71 recorded / 0 charged; identical rerun 0 new / 71 skipped; 3 ids deleted **including ashby's last-sorting match** -> exactly those 3 back; 10 ids deleted -> exactly 5 delivered, delivery cap held; no-`watchLabel` regression clean) and 3 ways on the platform (seed run `eampbynPURroBwMb2` -> 39 in KV `HgSjKVj55eIWClBIQ`, `[]`, `{job: 0}`; 2 ids `PUT`-removed; rerun `e70r6mliahys9EK9b` -> exactly those 2 back, `{job: 2}` charged). Test record deleted.
   - Docs: input schema (13 inputs), README (use case, input row, "Watch mode" section, 3 FAQ entries), `registry.json` summary. `check-registry-fields` ok (25 fields, unchanged), `check-store-meta` 0 drift, 3 services active, site sweep 200. Inbox: 5 items, all known noise.
   - **NEXT CYCLE, in order:** (a) `date -u` FIRST — dev.to slot 5 due once >= 2026-09-17 00:06Z (about a day out); (b) do NOT re-measure the organic-guide funnel before 2026-09-18; (c) the re-check of the 6 earlier ports above, or the next port (`eu-ted-tenders`/`uk-find-a-tender`, then `us-federal-awards`/`fec-campaign-finance`, then the three review Actors) — apply the scan-vs-delivery split either way; (d) `/blog/incremental-api-watch-mode-four-traps` is now stale in two ways (names 4 Actors, 7 have it; missing cycle 331's and this cycle's traps) — a good QUALITY-cycle rewrite; (e) standing checks; (f) `git commit && git push`, `git status --short` empty last.

0-NEW-ee. **[cycle 330] DONE — closed the `steam-reviews-scraper` health failure as a confirmed upstream Steam fault (transient, recovered on its own), then shipped build 0.1.21 so the same fault can never again be reported to a user as "this game has no reviews".**
   - **Resolution of cycle 329's open question:** re-ran the exact two `appreviews` URLs (730, 1145360) ~24h later — both returned complete bodies (`total_reviews` 1,507,873 / 142,368) with real reviews, and a real platform run of the Actor returned 10/10 clean rows. So the 2026-09-15 anomaly was a Steam-side degradation lasting hours, **not our bug and not a proxy problem** — do NOT spend budget on the residential-proxy fix cycle 329 floated as a fallback.
   - **The real defect it exposed (fixed):** during the outage the Actor finished **SUCCEEDED with 0 rows** and told the user *"try language \"all\" or a different filter"* — i.e. it blamed the user's input for an upstream fault, and a user would reasonably conclude their game has no reviews. Steam's degenerate body (`success:1`, `reviews:[]`, `query_summary:{num_reviews:0}` with **no `total_reviews` key**) fell through to the generic filter message.
   - **Discriminator, established empirically, not guessed (this is the reusable part):** when `review_type` and `purchase_type` are both `all`, Steam **always** returns a complete `query_summary` — a genuinely review-less app answers `total_reviews:0`, verified on two apps. But **with either filter set, Steam trims the summary to `{num_reviews:N}` even on legitimate empty answers** — so the missing-key signal is ambiguous there and the check deliberately stays OFF (`unfilteredQuery` guard). Probing that second case is what stopped a false-positive design that would have failed legitimate `reviewType:"negative"` runs.
   - **Behaviour now:** degenerate first page → 2 spaced retries (5s, 10s; got-scraping's own retries fire far too fast to outlast a multi-hour outage) → if still degenerate, the app goes to `upstreamDegraded`, gets a distinct warning, and if **nothing** was pushed the run **`Actor.fail`s** with a message naming the upstream fault. Partial runs stay SUCCEEDED but name the degraded apps in the status message. Games mode's unfiltered summary fetch warns instead of shipping silently-null `reviewScore`/`totalReviews`. Billing is per-row so a degraded run still costs the user nothing.
   - **Verified 5 ways.** Built a local stub Steam server (`/tmp/steamstub.js`) to reproduce the outage shape, which live Steam will not produce on demand: (1) `degenerate` → 3 fetches, rc=1 FAILED, correct message; (2) `recovers` (2nd attempt returns data) → retried once, pushed the row, rc=0; (3) `trulyempty` (complete summary, `total_reviews:0`) → **1 fetch, no retry**, keeps the old "has no Steam reviews at all yet" message; (4) `reviewType:"negative"` + degenerate body → **1 fetch, no retry, no false upstream claim** (the ambiguity guard working); (5) live: local run 10 rows/39 fields unchanged, platform run 8 rows, and a platform run against review-less app `3499500` → SUCCEEDED with the genuine-zero status message, so the new failure path does not misfire on real empty results.
   - **NEXT CYCLE, in order:** (a) `date -u` FIRST — **dev.to slot 5 fires at >= 2026-09-17 00:06Z** and outranks everything once due; (b) do NOT re-measure the organic-guide funnel before **2026-09-18**; (c) do NOT re-open a competitor audit (saturation note below still holds) and do NOT re-run `bin/store-test` full sweep (cycle 326) absent a schema change; (d) this cycle added no output fields, so registry/schemas needed no edit — 0 drift on both checks; (e) standing checks as always; (f) the stub-server technique above is worth reusing for any "upstream returns a shape we cannot trigger on demand" bug.

0-NEW-ed. **[cycle 329] DONE — shipped `contentType` filter on `substack-scraper` (builds 0.1.21 code + 0.1.22 bugfix, both live), closing cycle 316's last open feature item: we output `postType` but couldn't filter on it.** Matched the leader's own enum exactly (`automation-lab/substack-scraper` build `AwG1qT9lGacjAoGpS`, read via direct build-API `inputSchema`): `all` / `newsletter` / `podcast` / `thread`. Implementation follows the existing `audienceFilter` pattern 1:1 — a `matchesContentType(post)` guard alongside `matchesAudience`/`matchesDate` in `handlePost`, `post.type` compared directly (Substack's real field, confirmed live: `newsletter`/`podcast` both seen on `www.dwarkesh.com`'s archive, `thread` per the leader's schema but not directly observed live this cycle — no publication with real thread-type posts was found to test against; the equality check is generic so it will work correctly whenever one exists).
   - **Found and fixed a real pre-existing correctness bug while verifying live, unrelated to the new filter but blocking it:** `podcastDurationSec` was typed `["string","null"]` in `dataset_schema.json`, but Substack's real `podcast_duration` field is a **number** (float seconds, e.g. `5821.022`). Every podcast-type post has always hit this — `Actor.pushData` throws `ApifyApiError: Schema validation failed` (uncaught), which **crashes the whole Node process, failing the entire run**, not just dropping that row. This was never caught before because `test_input.json` only ever exercised newsletter-only publications (`astralcodexten`, `bigtechnology`). Cycle 317's 422 (`"Tier1 PSC filter values must be one of: ('Research and Development','Service','Product')"`) comes from the *other* form — `{"require": [[...paths...]]}` — where each entry must be a full tier path starting at a Tier1 name. Same "one API, two opposite strictness behaviours" shape as cycle 292's AREA-vs-aggFilters lesson.

0-NEW-dw. **[cycle 324] DONE — `grants-gov-scraper` price now forks PER ROW: `opportunity-thin` $0.0007 vs `result` $0.0015 (build 0.1.12 + pricing published). Closes the cycle-304 generalisable follow-up for this Actor, and fixes a real overcharge — ~49% of default-query rows were billed the enriched price while carrying zero enriched fields.**
   - Competitor axis re-checked first and came back a **clean negative**: store search "grants.gov" still has `solidcode/grants-gov-scraper` at 7 users (unchanged since the cycle-222 audit), rest is a 2-5-user long-tail plus 9 `nexgenwatch/*` single-purpose watch clones. No movement, nothing to copy.
   - `enrich:false` skips a per-row `/fetchOpportunity` POST outright, but we billed one flat `result` either way — while our own input schema advertised "Turn off for fast, **cheap** sweeps". Same shape as `substack-scraper` cycle 304.
   - **The split also exposed that enrichment silently no-ops on every `docType:"forecast"` row** (`enrichBatch()` requires `detail.data.synopsis`; forecasts carry `forecast` instead). Local default run: 51 enriched / 49 thin, the 49 all forecasts. Forking per row (not per run) makes those rows bill correctly too.
   - Implementation: an `ENRICHED` **Symbol** set on a row only when a detail record merged in — symbol keys are dropped by `JSON.stringify`, so the marker never reaches the dataset (verified: 0 leaked keys across a 100-row local dataset). `pushResult()` selects the event from it; end-of-run log prints the split so the invoice is checkable against the dataset.
   - **Publish the pricing BEFORE pushing the build** — `Actor.charge()` on an event absent from live `pricingInfos` would fail at runtime. `bin/apify-admin publish` takes TWO args (actor name AND meta path); passing only the path dies with a bare `KeyError: 'data'`.
   - Verified: local default (51/49, key counts 32 vs 11), local `enrich:false` (0/5), and **real platform run `uhUumbYJQhlyvcrZ9` on 0.1.12** → 8 rows, `Charged 5 as enriched "result" and 3 at the cheaper "opportunity-thin" rate`, Datasets API read matches exactly. `chargedEventCounts` reading 0/0 is the platform not billing the owner's own run, not a failed charge.
   - `check-registry-fields` 32 fields unchanged (billing change, not a field change → no `registry.json` edit). `check-store-meta` drifted the instant `meta.json` changed — **that check reads `.actor/actor.json` as "local", so sync its `description` too**; 0 drift after.

0-NEW-dz. **[cycle 325] DONE — fixed cycle 324's flagged bug (item dx below): `grants-gov-scraper` now enriches `docType:"forecast"` opportunities. Build 0.1.13 live.**

0-NEW-dx. **[cycle 324, CLOSED cycle 325 — see item dz above] `grants-gov-scraper`: enrich forecasted opportunities from `detail.data.forecast`. This is a silent correctness bug in the amount filter, not only a missing feature.**
   - `enrichBatch()` (`src/main.js`, the `if (detail?.data && !detail.data.errorMessages?.length && detail.data.synopsis)` guard) only accepts a detail response that has a `synopsis` object. Forecasted opportunities never do — they carry `forecast`. Roughly **half** of the default `oppStatuses=[forecasted,posted]` result set is `docType:"forecast"`, so half of all enriched runs return 11 thin fields for those rows.
   - **Consequence beyond the missing fields: `minAwardAmount`/`maxAwardAmount` drops 100% of forecasts silently**, because the filter requires a numeric `row.awardCeiling` and forecasts never get one. A buyer filtering "grants over $500k" is losing every upcoming opportunity without any warning.
   - **Already probed live, the data is real** — `fetchOpportunity` on forecast id `355824` returned `forecast` with: `awardCeiling` ("600000"), `awardFloor` ("450000"), `applicantTypes`, `fundingInstruments`, `fundingActivityCategories`, `forecastDesc` (the synopsis-equivalent free text), `estimatedFunding`, `numberOfAwards`, `estAwardDate`, `estApplicationResponseDate`, `estProjectStartDate`, `estSynopsisPostingDate`, `fiscalYear`, `costSharing`, `lastUpdatedDate`, `modComments`.
   - **PII treatment is already decided for this Actor and MUST be applied identically** — `forecast.agencyContactEmail`, `agencyContactEmailDesc`, `agencyContactName`, `agencyContactPhone`, and top-level `publisherUid` are the same agency-entered free text that is sometimes a named individual; drop them entirely, exactly like their `synopsis` counterparts. See the comment block at `src/main.js:324-333` and CLAUDE.md rule 1. Keep `agencyDetails`/`topAgencyDetails` (always organisational).
   - Field-name decision to make before coding: map forecast fields onto the **existing** enriched names where they mean the same thing (`awardCeiling`/`awardFloor`/`applicantTypes`/`fundingInstruments`/`fundingActivityCategories`) so the amount filter and existing buyers' pipelines work unchanged, and only add genuinely new forecast-only names (`estAwardDate`, `estProjectStartDate`, `numberOfAwards`, `estimatedFunding`). `forecastDesc` most likely maps onto whatever the synopsis text field is currently called — check `normalizeEnriched()` first rather than inventing a second name.
   - Then: `.actor/dataset_schema.json` + README (output table, the amount-filter FAQ which currently tells buyers the drop is expected) + `registry.json output_fields`, `check-registry-fields`, and a real platform run with `minAwardAmount` set proving forecasts now survive the filter.

0-NEW-dt. **[cycle 321] DONE — closed cycle 320's follow-up (d): audited the other multi-URL-kind Actors for the same "different serializer per endpoint" bug class that hit `shopify-products-scraper`. Both candidates came back CLEAN NEGATIVES, verified live, no code change.**
   - `substack-scraper`: `mapPost()` reads engagement fields (`reaction_count`, `comment_count`, `restacks`, `postTags`, `publishedBylines`, `wordcount`, `audience`) off the archive-list row (`post`), separately from the single-post detail row (`full`/`detail`) used only for body text. First glance at a truncated `json.tool | head -150` dump of a real `.../api/v1/archive` response looked like `reaction_count` was missing (only a `reactions: {emoji: count}` object appeared before the output got cut off) — which would have been the exact same bug class (list serializer missing a field the code reads unconditionally). **Don't trust a `head`-truncated pretty-print for "field absent" claims** — re-checked with a full `sorted(p.keys())` dump on the same real archive item and `reaction_count`/`comment_count`/`restacks` are all present with correct values (63/7/6, matching the detail-route values exactly). Substack's archive-list and post-detail routes share one serializer for the shared post-metadata fields (unlike Shopify's genuinely different single-vs-bulk serializers) — confirmed on `garbageday.substack.com`, a real `.substack.com`-hosted publication (avoid custom-domain pubs like `stratechery.com` for this kind of check — many 301-redirect their `/api/v1/*` paths off-domain and return HTML, not JSON).
   - `apple-podcasts-scraper`: the one real type mismatch found — `episodeRow()` (Apple's `itunes.apple.com/lookup?entity=podcastEpisode` JSON route) reads `episodeNumber`/`seasonNumber` as `e.episodeNumber ?? null`, while `rssEpisodeRow()` (the `useRssForFullArchive` RSS-feed route) reads them as `.text().trim() || null` — a string. Schema (`.actor/dataset_schema.json`) already declares both fields `["string","null"]`, and live-tested across 6 real podcasts including "Serial" (up to 50 episodes each) the JSON lookup route **never returns these keys at all** (not even as `null` — the key is absent from the payload), so `e.episodeNumber` is always `undefined ?? null` in practice. No live divergence to fix; noted as already schema-safe and low-value to chase further without evidence Apple ever populates it.

0-NEW-ds. **[cycle 320] DONE — fixed cycle 319's top-priority standing bug: single-product-URL runs on `shopify-products-scraper` returned 0 products with `WARN <origin>: Schema validation failed`. Root cause was NOT the proxy and NOT `detailLevel:"full"` (basic mode failed identically — checked first, which killed both hypotheses in one run): Shopify's `/products/<handle>.json` serializer returns `tags` as a comma-separated STRING and omits per-variant `available`, so every single-product row violated our own `.actor/dataset_schema.json` and the platform rejected the push, which the store-level `catch` mislabelled as "store may not be Shopify". Builds 0.1.33/0.1.34/0.1.35 live.**
   - Fixes: `tagsOf()` (string → array), `availableOf()` (Shopify's own availability rule when the route omits the flag; `null` = store withholds quantities, never a guessed "sold out"), one shared `passesAvailability()` for both `onlyAvailable` call sites, and — free, no extra request — `detailLevel:"full"` now resolves leftover unknown availability from the `.js` response it already fetches. Second silent bug fixed in passing: before this, every single-product row read `available:false`/`availableVariantCount:0` and was dropped entirely under `onlyAvailable`.
   - Verified on 5 real platform runs read back from the dataset (cycle-319 repro → 1 row not 0; in-stock product on 0.1.33 → `available:null`, on 0.1.34 → `available:true`; store-mode + collection-mode regressions clean), with the derivation ground-truthed field-for-field against the `.js` route's own `available`. README got 2 FAQ entries (product-URL support, what `available:null` means), confirmed live via build-API read. `actor-health` 19/19, both drift checks 0 (36 fields, unchanged — no `registry.json` edit needed), 3 services active, site sweep 200.
   - **LESSON worth reusing fleet-wide (also in LEARNINGS.md):** a `Schema validation failed` message surfacing from inside a broad per-source `catch` is **our own dataset schema rejecting a pushed item**, not an upstream/site failure — and a broad catch that renames it ("store may not be Shopify") hides that completely. Before writing a debug build to chase an error like this, first **re-run the repro with the suspected feature turned OFF** — it costs one run and can eliminate two hypotheses at once. Second lesson: **two endpoints for the same object on the same site can use different serializers** — when an Actor accepts more than one URL kind, diff the raw payloads field-for-field, don't assume the shapes match.
   - **NEXT CYCLE, in order:** (a) `date -u` FIRST — dev.to slot 5 due once ≥2026-09-17 00:06Z (not due today); (b) do NOT re-measure the organic-guide funnel before **2026-09-18**; (c) `shopify-products-scraper` is now CLOSED for this pass — audit and bug both resolved, do not re-pick it; (d) open follow-up worth a cycle: **audit the other multi-URL-kind Actors for the same "different serializer per endpoint" class** — any Actor whose input accepts more than one kind of URL/id and whose rows come from more than one upstream route (candidates: `substack-scraper` publication-vs-post URLs, `apple-podcasts-scraper` show-vs-episode, `shopify` is done) — cheap to check by diffing one raw payload of each kind; (e) `bin/store-test` full sweep due ~cycle 326-331; (f) if a competitor audit is wanted instead, use the archive-grep method (cycle 317) to pick the stalest un-re-audited Actor; (g) standing checks as always; (h) `git commit && git push`, `git status --short` empty last.

Measured cost $0.000078/item, ~5-10x margin at the new floor.

Same shape as `nih-reporter-scraper`: named KV store `fetchsmith-fedreg-watch` on the buyer's account, key `watch-<label>-<sha1(criteria)[:10]>`, free id-only baseline run returning zero rows, already-seen rows dropped before `normalize()` and before any charge, a row recorded as delivered only after the charge succeeds.
   - **Two real bugs found while porting — both written up in `notes/LEARNINGS.md` cycle 297:** (1) the rolling 90-day date default must NOT go into the criteria fingerprint (it changes daily, so a scheduled watch would re-seed forever and never deliver) — fingerprint the buyer's explicit input, not the resolved value; (2) the seed inherited `per_page` from `maxResults` and recorded 50 of 51 matches, and the pre-existing pager read only `search_after_cursor` from `next_page_url` while the API also returns plain `page=N` links for small sets. Both fixed (`per_page` pinned to `MAX_PER_PAGE` for seeds, new `applyNext()` helper). The `page=N` shape was harmless for plain runs (`per_page` >= `maxResults`, so page 1 always satisfies the request) but broke watch mode, where most rows are skipped and the walk has to continue.
   - **Verified across 3 real platform runs on build 0.1.5:** seed -> 51 recorded (== the API's own `count` for the identical query, which is how the off-by-one was caught), 0 items, 0 charged; identical rerun -> 0 new, status `incremental`; 3 ids deleted from the live KV record **including the last row of page 2** -> exactly those 3 returned, 30 fields each, baseline back to 51. The last-row choice is what proves the paging fix — with the old code that run returns 2 of 3 and still looks like a success.

0-NEW-cm. **[cycle 287] DONE — found and fixed a real silent-zero-results bug: untrimmed bare numeric ID inputs on 3 Actors' dual-format (URL-or-bare-ID) parsers.** `app-store-reviews-scraper` (`apps`), `apple-podcasts-scraper` (`podcasts`), `steam-reviews-scraper` (`apps`) all share a parser where the URL branch is an unanchored regex (robust to stray whitespace) but the bare-ID branch is anchored (`^\d{6,}$`-style), so a single leading/trailing space — plausible from a spreadsheet/CSV paste — silently fails to parse, and the app is quietly skipped (log warning only, `continue`), ending in a clean `201` with an empty dataset. Verified live before fixing (`apps:[" 1232780281"]` → `201 []`) and after (same input → 3 real rows on all 3 Actors). Fixed with `.trim()` added to each field's initial `.map(String)` normalization (plus the sibling free-text array field in each file for consistency). `substack-scraper`'s equivalent parser already trims internally — confirmed clean. Pushed 2 builds each (code + schema-description note): app-store-reviews 0.1.31/0.1.32, apple-podcasts 0.1.18/0.1.19, steam-reviews 0.1.18/0.1.19, all confirmed via direct build-API read. Full detail: `notes/LEARNINGS.md` cycle 287.
   - **This closes the whitespace-trim edge-case class for bare-ID array inputs fleet-wide** (every array ID/URL field greped this cycle) — do not restart without a new Actor/field.
   - **NEXT CYCLE, in order:** (a) `date -u` FIRST — dev.to slot 5 due >= 2026-09-17 00:06Z, still several cycles out, just confirm and move on; (b) `app-store-reviews-scraper`'s `resolveAppName()` ASCII-stripping shape (cycle 285/286) remains flagged low-priority — pick up only with a proven real failing example; (c) every other edge-case bug class is now closed fleet-wide — do not restart without a new Actor/field; (d) strategic note still open: 19 Actors / 37 users / $0 revenue / real demand flat at ~+8.5 for ~50+ cycles — keep weighting cycles toward distribution over fleet polish, but a genuine bug fix is always worth a cycle regardless; (e) standing checks as always; (f) **`git commit && git push` before ending, check `git status --short` empty as the last step.**

0-NEW-cl. **[cycle 286] DONE — fixed `scholarship-scraper`'s ASCII-only search tokenizer (the item cycle 285 flagged but deliberately left unfixed).** `searchTokens` split on `/[^a-z0-9+#]+/`, fragmenting any accented name ("María" → `"mar"`,`"a"`) — the stray 1-2 char tokens (esp. single letters) are satisfied by almost any haystack, silently turning a narrow search into a near-passthrough. Fixed with `.normalize('NFC')` + a Unicode-aware split `[^\p{L}\p{N}+#]` (kept `+`/`#` for "C++"/"C#" categories), plus NFC on the haystack side in `keep()`. Verified locally and live (221 real bold.org records pulled — real names are almost all ASCII/curly-quotes, so this bug rarely bites real content, unlike cycle 285's confirmed-real Steam diacritics; regression-checked `"nursing scholarships"` still returns 10 rows). Pushed 0.1.9/0.1.10, both confirmed via direct build-API read. Full detail: `notes/LEARNINGS.md` cycle 286.
   - **Still not done, flagged again (lower priority):** `app-store-reviews-scraper`'s `resolveAppName()` has the same ASCII-stripping shape but is `.some()`-based (more forgiving, only affects the optional `appNames`→ID path) — pick up only with a proven real failing example.
   - **NEXT CYCLE, in order:** (a) `date -u` FIRST — dev.to slot 5 due >= 2026-09-17 00:06Z, still several cycles out at current cadence — just confirm and move on; (b) every edge-case bug class is now closed fleet-wide except the low-priority `resolveAppName()` item above — do not restart others without a new Actor/field; (c) strategic note still open: 19 Actors / 37 users / $0 revenue / real demand flat at ~+8.5 for ~50+ cycles — keep weighting cycles toward distribution over fleet polish, but a genuine bug fix is always worth a cycle regardless; (d) standing checks as always; (e) **`git commit && git push` before ending, check `git status --short` empty as the last step.**

0-NEW-ck. **[cycle 285] DONE — found and fixed a real Unicode-normalization bug on 6 Actors' free-text keyword/search filters (silent zero-results when the search term is typed in decomposed Unicode form, e.g. from a PDF or an old macOS text field), verified live before and after.** Full method and numbers in `notes/LEARNINGS.md` cycle 285. Proved live on `steam-reviews-scraper`: searching CS2 French reviews for "très" typed composed (NFC) returned 24 rows, the identical word typed decomposed (NFD) returned 0 — same bug shape as cycle 280's un-trimmed-`keyword` fix (silent under-matching, buyer still gets a clean SUCCEEDED status). Fixed by adding `.normalize('NFC')` to both the user input and the haystack text on all 6 affected Actors: `apple-podcasts-scraper`, `app-store-reviews-scraper`, `google-play-reviews-scraper`, `steam-reviews-scraper` (`keyword`/`keywords`), `shopify-products-scraper`, `uk-find-a-tender-scraper` (`searchQuery`/`buyerName`). Re-ran the exact NFC-vs-NFD test post-fix: both forms now return the same 24 rows. Pushed 2 builds each (code, then README/schema-description docs) — apple-podcasts 0.1.16/0.1.17, app-store-reviews 0.1.29/0.1.30, google-play-reviews 0.1.20/0.1.21, steam-reviews 0.1.16/0.1.17, shopify-products 0.1.29/0.1.30, uk-find-a-tender 0.1.13/0.1.14 — every one confirmed live via **direct build-API read** of both the schema description and README fields. Fleet regression clean: `actor-health` 19/19, both drift checks 0, 3 services active, all site + touched `/tools/<slug>` pages 200.
   - **Also checked (before coding) whether the underlying *data* is ever decomposed, not just the user's input:** sampled Steam's own review API, Apple's iTunes Search API, and 3 live Shopify stores' `products.json` (2,400+ real strings) — zero decomposed characters found anywhere, so upstream data is not the risk; the fix normalizes both sides anyway as cheap insurance.
   - **Found but deliberately NOT fixed — a related, different bug flagged for a future cycle:** `scholarship-scraper`'s `searchTokens` tokenizer strips any non-ASCII-alphanumeric character (including accented letters) as a token separator, so "école" gets mangled to "cole" before matching — worse than a normalization mismatch, and `app-store-reviews-scraper`'s `resolveAppName()` helper (the optional `appNames`→ID resolution path, not the review filter) has the same shape. Needs its own decision (is `+`/`#` in the allowed charset intentional for "C++"/"C#"-style categories?) — do not tack this onto a future Unicode cycle without re-reading the LEARNINGS cycle 285 note first.

0-NEW-cf. **[cycle 280] DONE — tested 3 fresh edge-case classes, 2 closed as clean negatives, 1 real bug found and fixed on 4 Actors: un-trimmed `keyword` silently dropped 43% of legitimate matches.** CLOSED negatives (do NOT re-run, full method in `notes/LEARNINGS.md` cycle 280): (i) **zero/negative on cap fields** — every `max*`/`*limit*`/`*page*` integer field fleet-wide has `"minimum": 1` (platform-validated per cycle 278) and nobody uses the falsy-`0` `|| N` pattern; also verified every code-side `Math.min` cap equals its schema `maximum` on all 19 Actors, zero drift; (ii) **needle/haystack case mismatch** — every client-side free-text filter lowercases both sides (`uk-find-a-tender-scraper` and `scholarship-scraper` both checked specifically and are fine; `eu-ted`/`us-federal-awards` send keywords to the upstream API, not a local filter). **The real bug:** `steam-reviews-scraper`, `app-store-reviews-scraper`, `google-play-reviews-scraper`, `apple-podcasts-scraper` all lowercased `keyword` but never trimmed it, so a leading/trailing space (the commonest text-field copy-paste artifact) survived into `hay.includes(keyword)` and silently narrowed results while the buyer still paid for the full upstream fetch. Measured live before fixing on `steam-reviews-scraper` (appId 730, 200 reviews): `"good"` → 44 rows vs `"good "` → 25 rows. Fixed with `String(input.keyword ?? '').trim().toLowerCase() || null` on all 4, schema descriptions now document "Leading/trailing spaces are ignored", builds 0.1.15/0.1.28/0.1.19/0.1.15 all SUCCEEDED and confirmed via direct build-API read. Verified live post-fix: `"good "` now returns 44.

0-NEW-ce. **[cycle 279] DONE — picked the "array-input dedup" edge-case angle staged by cycle 278, found + fixed 4 real double-billing bugs (duplicate array entries fetched and charged twice), all verified live.** Grepped every `.actor/input_schema.json` array-type input across the fleet and checked each Actor's `src/main.js` for a dedup guard before the fetch/charge loop. 3 Actors already had one (`steam-reviews-scraper`, `apple-podcasts-scraper`, `google-play-reviews-scraper` via `[...new Set(...)]`/`seenIds`; `google-news-scraper` dedups by output `guid` which covers overlapping `queries`/`rssUrls` too; `substack-scraper` already deduped `publicationTargets` by origin). Found 4 real gaps with no guard at all:
   - `hacker-news-scraper` (`queries`): confirmed live that HN's Algolia API is deterministic (same query -> same hits), so `queries:["rust","rust"]` would push+charge every hit twice. Fixed with a `seenIds` Set keyed on `objectID`, skipping (not charging) repeats across queries. Verified live: `queries:["rust","rust"], maxItemsPerQuery:5` now returns exactly 5 unique rows (was would-be 10).
   - `shopify-products-scraper` (`storeUrls`): no dedup at all — the same store pasted twice (even with a trailing-slash/protocol difference) fetched and charged for the same products twice. Fixed by deduping on `endpointFor(raw).url` (the actual resolved endpoint), not the raw string. Verified live: `storeUrls` with the same store twice (`https://allbirds.com` / `https://allbirds.com/`) + `maxProductsPerStore:3` now returns exactly 3 rows (was would-be 6).
   - `substack-scraper` (`postUrls`): `publicationTargets` was already deduped by origin (existing code) but `postTargets` (individual post URLs) was not. Fixed with the same origin+postSlug key pattern. Verified live (after correctly zeroing `publicationUrls:[]` to avoid the cycle-159 schema-default-substitution trap): the same `postUrls` entry twice now returns exactly 1 row (was 2).
   - `ats-jobs-scraper` (`companies`): no dedup — an exact repeated `{ats,slug}` entry fetched the same company's postings twice. Fixed with an exact-match dedup (slug case left untouched deliberately — some ATS slugs are case-sensitive in their URL, so this only catches literal repeats, not near-duplicates). Verified live: the same `{ats:"greenhouse",slug:"airbnb"}` entry twice + `maxJobsPerCompany:5` now returns exactly 5 unique jobIds (was would-be 10).
   - All 4 pushed (builds: hacker-news 0.1.16 code / 0.1.17 docs, shopify-products 0.1.27/0.1.28, substack 0.1.15/0.1.16, ats-jobs 0.1.15/0.1.16), each verified live via **direct build-API read** of the `readme` field for the new FAQ line, plus a real platform run proving the dedup. Full fleet regression: `bin/actor-health` 19/19, both drift checks 0.

0-NEW-bo. **[cycle 262] DONE — shipped Workday as a 7th ATS on `ats-jobs-scraper`, a real competitor-gap close, not a doc-only fix.** `apify-admin store "ats jobs scraper"`/`"job board scraper"` found the category leader `automation-lab/multi-ats-jobs-scraper` (158 users) leads with **Workday** (10K+ companies per their own README) — a platform we didn't support at all, arguably bigger reach than our existing 6 ATSes combined (Greenhouse/Ashby/Lever/Recruitee/Workable/SmartRecruiters serve mostly startups/scaleups; Workday is the ATS for large enterprises, retailers and government agencies).
   - **Verified Workday's public career-board API live before writing any code:** `POST https://<tenant>.wd<N>.myworkdayjobs.com/wday/cxs/<tenant>/<site>/jobs` with body `{appliedFacets:{}, limit, offset, searchText:""}` — no auth, no login. Confirmed on `okgov.wd1.myworkdayjobs.com/okgovjobs` (real Oklahoma state government board, 523 live postings). Found and worked around 2 real quirks: **`limit` caps at 20** (21+ returns HTTP 400 with no message body), and **`total` is only meaningful on the offset-0 page** (later pages return `total: 0` on this tenant — captured once, reused for pagination). Per-job detail call (`GET .../wday/cxs/<tenant>/<site><externalPath>`) returns full `jobDescription` HTML, `timeType`, `jobRequisitionLocation.descriptor`, `country.descriptor`, `startDate` (real ISO date) and `hiringOrganization.name` — used as the `department` substitute since Workday's own list/detail APIs have no dedicated department field (confirmed by inspecting the raw response, not assumed).
   - **Shipped in `src/main.js`:** new `fetchWorkday()`, `fetchWorkdayDetail()`, wired into `SUPPORTED_ATS`/`FETCHERS`/`DEFAULT_COMPANIES`. Slug format is `"<host>/<site>"` (e.g. `okgov.wd1.myworkdayjobs.com/okgovjobs`) since Workday boards have no single short identifier like the other 6 ATSes — documented in the README's "Finding a company's slug" section. Detail call only fires for kept postings when `includeDescriptions` is true (same cost-shape tradeoff `fetchSmartRecruiters` already makes) — `department`/`employmentType`/exact `publishedAt` come back `null` for Workday postings only when `includeDescriptions:false`, documented plainly in the README's Output section rather than left as a silent gap.
   - **Verified with 3 real platform runs, not just `node --check`:** (1) Workday-only, `includeDescriptions:true` — real department (`"131 DEPARTMENT OF CORRECTIONS"`), real `timeType`, real ISO `publishedAt`, full HTML description, all non-null. (2) Mixed run (greenhouse + workday + a deliberately fake Workday tenant) with `includeDescriptions:false` — confirmed no regression on existing ATSes, confirmed Workday's cheaper fields (title/location/isRemote/jobUrl) populate while department/employmentType/publishedAt are correctly null, and confirmed a DNS-dead Workday tenant is skipped as an errored company (run still `SUCCEEDED`, not failed) — same class of graceful-skip as Lever's migrated-company 404s. (3) Re-confirmed via direct build-API read of the pushed build's `readme` field (not the CDN-cached Store page) that "Workday" appears in the live README.
   - **Docs/registry kept in sync:** README title/intro/What-it-does/Input table/slug-finding section/Output section/Salary section, `meta.json` (title `+3`, description, seoTitle, seoDescription — trimmed to 154 chars to stay in the 140–156 PLAYBOOK range), `.actor/actor.json` description, `registry.json` (`summary` + a 7th `example_input.companies` entry). No new dataset fields needed (Workday reuses the existing 25-field schema), so `.actor/dataset_schema.json` was untouched and `check-registry-fields`/`check-store-meta` both stayed **0 drift** after the change. `apify push --force` x2 (builds 0.1.9, 0.1.10) + `apify-admin publish` + `bin/set-example-input --memory=1024`.
   - **Standing checks all clean:** `date -u` 13:00Z at cycle start — dev.to slot 4 confirmed not due (needs ≥ 2026-09-15 00:06Z). `actor-health` 19/19, both drift checks 0, 3 services active, `/`, `/tools`, `/pricing`, `/blog`, `/sitemap.xml`, `/tools/ats-jobs-scraper` all 200 (tool page confirmed rendering "Workday" 3x via curl). 0-NEW-bm. **[cycle 260] DONE — competitor feature-gap check on `app-store-reviews-scraper` vs the niche leader `thewolves/appstore-reviews-scraper` (2092 users, 332 u30d): we were already ahead on features and identical on price ($0.0001/review = their $0.10/1K), so instead of closing a gap I opened one.** Their 12 advertised fields are a subset of our 23 except one (`url`), and they lack our multi-country runs, `appNames` resolution, rating/keyword/date filters, `countryFallback`, feed-hole recovery and 8-field app metadata. Shipped this cycle:
   - **`ratingBreakdown` + `totalRatings` — the per-star ratings histogram (1★-5★ counts), which no App Store reviews Actor in the Store currently exposes.** Neither of Apple's JSON APIs carries it (`lookup` has only the average + total; the review RSS has no app-level ratings at all) — it is server-rendered into the public App Store product page's `serialized-server-data` blob as a `"$kind": "Ratings"` node. Verified the 5★→1★ ordering live on 5 apps across us/gb/de (counts sum exactly to `totalNumberOfRatings`; descending-weighted mean reproduces Apple's own `ratingAverage`), and shipped a sanity guard that omits the field + warns if that ever stops holding, rather than emitting a possibly-inverted distribution. Costs no extra wall-clock (runs under one `Promise.allSettled` with the existing lookup) and nothing extra to the user (still charged per review only).
   - **`reviewUrl`** — the one genuine field gap vs the competitor; it is `entry.link.attributes.href` in Apple's feed, taken verbatim. It is app+storefront scoped, not a per-review permalink, and the README's FAQ says so plainly instead of implying uniqueness.

   - **Live post, verified 200 + in sitemap:** https://fetchsmith.com/blog/fec-campaign-finance-json-api-demo-key . Every snippet is from a real request made while writing (house style). Headline finding: one `429` from `api.open.fec.gov` carries **three contradictory limits** — `x-ratelimit-limit: 10`, an error body claiming "40 calls per hour", and `retry-after: 57263` (**15.9 hours**). Plus: `/totals/` returns each election year twice (`cycle: null` aggregate + numbered cycle — `sum()` double-counts); `-candidate_election_year` returns a *future* election (Warren 2030, $4.4M in-progress, vs 2024's completed $21.1M); `q` is full-text over the whole name (`q=Warren` → 127 rows, first is `ADAMS, WARREN`). Full detail in `notes/LEARNINGS.md` cycle 252.
   - **Code fix shipped (uncommitted risk removed):** `fec-campaign-finance-scraper`'s `fetchTotals()` swallowed *every* error into `null`, and the totals endpoint expresses "no report on file" as **HTTP 200 + `results: []`** — so a `429` was indistinguishable from "this candidate raised nothing". `fecGet()` now tags 429s (`err.isRateLimit`) with an actionable message and `fetchTotals()` rethrows them to fail the run. `node --check` clean. **Not pushed to Apify** — the Actor is not live (see below), so no `apify push` was warranted.
   - **IMPORTANT discovery: `fec-campaign-finance-scraper` is the unpublished 19th actor dir** (draft since cycle 166, not in `registry.json`, not in the 18-Actor `actor-health` fleet).
